<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Quillen's conjecture for all finite groups and all primes. More precisely, if a finite group <i>G</i> has trivial largest normal <i>p</i>-subgroup $`O_p(G)`$, then the poset of nontrivial elementary abelian <i>p</i>-subgroups of <i>G</i> has nonzero augmented reduced rational homology. This establishes the stronger rational-homology form of the conjecture.

- **claim-main-theorem** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every finite group $G$ and every prime $p$, if $O_p(G)=1$, then $$\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_p(G);\mathbb Q)\ne0.$$ Here reduced homology is augmented, so that the empty poset has $\widetilde H_{-1}(\varnothing;\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: Trivial largest normal p-subgroup forces nonzero augmented reduced rational homology of the poset of nonidentity elementary abelian p-subgroups, for every finite group and prime.
- **claim-reduction-two** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-reduction-two)): Let $G$ be a minimal-order counterexample to <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a> for $p=2$. Then $G$ has a simple component $L$ in the following list, where the field parameter $q$ has characteristic at least five: $$\begin{array}{ll} \mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_n(q),\ \mathop{\mathrm{PSU}}_n(q)& n\geq4,\\ \mathop{\mathrm{PSp}}_{2n}(q)& n\geq3,\\ \Omega_{2n+1}(q)& n\geq2,\\ \mathrm P\Omega_{2n}^{\pm}(q)& n\geq4. \end{array}$$
  - Explanation: At the prime two a minimal counterexample has a simple component from a listed set of classical groups over fields of characteristic at least five.
- **claim-reduction-odd** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-reduction-odd)): Suppose that, for every odd prime $p$, every odd prime power $q$ with $p\mid q+1$, and every simple $L=\mathop{\mathrm{PSU}}_n(q)$ with $n\geq5$, each elementary $p$-extension of $L$ inside $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ has the Quillen dimension property. Then <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a> holds for every finite group and every odd prime.
  - Explanation: The odd-prime case follows once every elementary extension of the listed unitary simple groups has the Quillen dimension property.
- **claim-component-core** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-component-core)): Let $G$ be a finite group, let $p$ be a prime, and let $L$ be a nonabelian simple component of $G$. If $O_p(G)=1$, then $$O_p(C_G(L))=1.$$
  - Explanation: Trivial p-core of the group gives trivial p-core of the centralizer of a nonabelian simple component.
- **claim-faithfulness-upgrade** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-faithfulness-upgrade)): Let $A$ be a faithful configuration for a nonabelian simple component $L$ of $G$. Suppose every element of order $p$ in $C_L(A)$ belongs to $A$. If $D\in\mathcal A_p(G)$ contains $A$, then $D\le N_G(L)$. Moreover, $$C_D(L)=1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad C_{LD}(L)=1.$$
  - Explanation: Elementary abelian overgroups of a faithful configuration normalize the component, and faithfulness transfers to the generated extension under the stated condition.
- **claim-saturated-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-saturated-residue)): Let $X$ be a subposet of $\mathcal A_p(G)$, and suppose $X$ contains the flag $$\sigma=[A_1<\cdots<A_r=A],\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(A_i)=i.$$ Define a linear map $$R_\sigma:\widetilde C_n(\Delta X;\mathbb Q) \longrightarrow \widetilde C_{n-r}(\Delta X_{>A};\mathbb Q)$$ as follows: a chain containing $\sigma$ is sent to its strict continuation after $A$, and every other chain is sent to zero. The continuation of $\sigma$ itself is $[\,]$. Chain groups in degrees below $-1$ are zero. Then $$\label{eq:residue-boundary} R_\sigma\partial=(-1)^r\partial R_\sigma.$$ In particular, residues of cycles are cycles, and residues of boundaries are boundaries, including in degree $-1$.
  - Explanation: The residue at a saturated flag anticommutes with the boundary up to sign, so residues of cycles are cycles and of boundaries are boundaries.
- **claim-product-shuffle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-shuffle)): With this notation there is a bilinear operation $$\boxtimes:\widetilde C_a(\Delta X;\mathbb Q) \otimes\widetilde C_b(\Delta Y;\mathbb Q) \longrightarrow \widetilde C_{a+b+1}(\Delta\mathcal P(H,K);\mathbb Q)$$ for $a,b\ge-1$, satisfying $$\label{eq:product-boundary} \partial(u\boxtimes v) =(\partial u)\boxtimes v+(-1)^{a+1}u\boxtimes\partial v.$$ It is the chain construction for the subdivision of the join $\Delta X*\Delta Y$ by the product poset. The empty chain is an identity for this operation.
  - Explanation: Chains on two commuting subgroup posets combine by a shuffle into chains on the product poset with a graded Leibniz boundary rule.
- **claim-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-propagation)): Let $G$ be a finite group and $p$ a prime. Assume that every proper subgroup $T<G$ with $O_p(T)=1$ satisfies $$\widetilde H_*(\Delta\mathcal A_p(T);\mathbb Q)\ne0.$$ Let $L$ be a nonabelian simple component of $G$ with $p\mid \|L\|$, and let $A$ be a faithful configuration for $L$ in $G$. Write $H=LA$ and $r=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(A)$. Assume the following three conditions.* 1. *Every element of order $p$ in $C_L(A)$ belongs to $A$.* 2. *There is no $D\in\mathcal A_p(G)$ with $D>A$ for which $LD$ acts faithfully on $L$ and $O_p(C_G(LD))=1$.* 3. *There is a cycle $\alpha\in\widetilde C_{r-1}(\Delta\mathcal A_p(H);\mathbb Q)$ whose coefficient on some flag $\sigma=[A_1<\cdots<A_r=A]$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(A_i)=i$, is nonzero.* *Then $\widetilde H_*(\Delta\mathcal A_p(G);\mathbb Q)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A cycle on a faithful component extension with a nonzero saturated-flag coefficient and two maximality conditions gives nonzero homology of the ambient group.
- **claim-maximal-family** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-maximal-family)): Assume the inductive hypothesis on proper subgroups of $G$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:propagation">9</a>, and let $L$ be a nonabelian simple component with $p\mid\|L\|$. Let $\mathcal F$ be a nonempty family of faithful configurations for $L$ in $G$ such that $$A\in\mathcal F,\quad D\in\mathcal A_p(G),\quad D>A, \quad D\text{ a faithful configuration} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad D\in\mathcal F.$$ Let $r$ be the largest rank of a member of $\mathcal F$. If some $A\in\mathcal F$ of rank $r$ is the final subgroup of a saturated flag having nonzero coefficient in a cycle of degree $r-1$ in $\Delta\mathcal A_p(LA)$, then $\widetilde H_*(\Delta\mathcal A_p(G);\mathbb Q)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A maximal-rank member of an overgroup-closed family of faithful configurations meets the propagation conditions.
- **claim-frame-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-frame-bound)): Let $W$ have dimension $h\geq2$ and one of the four structures above, and suppose that $W$ admits an allowed frame. The space $\mathcal Z(W)\subseteq\bigoplus_{\mathcal D\in\mathfrak F(W)}V_{\mathcal D}$ consists of cycles of degree $h-2$, each supported on full flags ending at sign groups of rank $h-1$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:frame-bound} \dim\mathcal Z(W)\ \geq\ F_h\,\|\mathfrak F(W)\|(h-1)!.$$ The following choices of $F_h$ are valid for each individual isometry type of $W$.* 1. *In the no-form case over $\mathbb F_u$, and in the hermitian case over $\mathbb F_{u^2}$, set $$\rho=\frac1{u(u+1)}\quad\hbox{and}\quad \rho=\frac1{u(u-1)}, \quad\hbox{respectively},\qquad y=\frac{1+\sqrt{1-4\rho}}2.$$ For $u\geq5$ one may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h\geq(17/25)^h$.* 2. *For a symmetric space over $\mathbb F_u$, with both line types allowed and $u\geq5$ odd, put $$x=\frac{1+\sqrt{1-4u/(u^2-1)}}2.$$ One may take $$\label{eq:symmetric-frame-fraction} F_h=x^h+(1-x)^h-\mathbf1_{2\mid h}\,2(u^2-1)^{-h/2} \ \geq\ (17/25)^h.$$ In particular $F_3\geq3/8$.* 3. *For a symmetric space over $\mathbb F_u$ with one fixed line type, where $u\geq7$ is odd, write $\chi$ for the quadratic character of $\mathbb F_u^\times$ and set $$\rho=\frac2{u-\chi(-1)},\qquad y=\frac{1+\sqrt{1-4\rho}}2.$$ One may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h>0$.* *The same statements hold for any realization by projective sign subgroups in which coarsened decompositions give the indicated subgroup flags and the complete flags ending at the full frames remain distinct.
  - Explanation: Assignments obeying the plane relations form a cycle space on full flags of full sign-group rank, with an explicit lower dimension bound.
- **claim-merger-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-merger-coefficients)): For a full flag in $\mathcal D(W)$ ending at a frame $\mathcal D$, complete its sequence of binary mergers by the final, implicit merger to the one-block partition. Its coefficient in $\sum_w f(w)a_w$ is, up to one sign depending on the flag and the chosen child orders, the evaluation of $f$ on the associated iterated superbracket. All iterated brackets on the letters of $\mathcal D$ occur in this way. Their span has dimension $(h-1)!$.
  - Explanation: Flag coefficients are evaluations on iterated superbrackets, all brackets occur, and their span has factorial dimension.
- **claim-frame-lie-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-lie-identification)): For every admissible $W$ of dimension at least two, the coefficient construction has image of dimension $\dim P(W)$: $$\dim\mathcal Z(W)=\dim P(W).$$
  - Explanation: The cycle space has the same dimension as the good component of the Lie superalgebra quotient.
- **claim-frame-marked-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-marked-factor)): The nonnegative series above satisfy $$\label{eq:frame-marked-factor} D\mathsf C\ \geq\ (D\mathsf P)\mathsf C$$ coefficientwise in every isometry type.
  - Explanation: A marking argument on leading module monomials gives a coefficientwise inequality for the relation-kernel series.
- **claim-frame-cherry-remark** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rem-frame-cherry)): The coefficient description gives a useful relation at every bottom pair of a merger tree. Fix all other leaves and the surrounding brackets. Replacing the bracket $[e_A,e_B]$ by the sum of these brackets over the unordered frames of the same plane gives zero in $P$, by <a href="#eq:frame-rq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:frame-rq">[eq:frame-rq]</a>. Every functional representing a member of $\mathcal Z(W)$ therefore satisfies this relation on the actual tree coefficients. For example, when $u=5$, a plane with square determinant has exactly one unordered frame of each homogeneous color, and a plane with nonsquare determinant has three unordered mixed frames. These counts follow from <a href="#eq:orthogonal-plane-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:orthogonal-plane-order">[eq:orthogonal-plane-order]</a>: the respective group orders are $8$ and $12$, and an ordered line-frame stabilizer has order $4$. For a homogeneous color one divides its two ordered frames by $2$; for a mixed frame the two possible orders have different color patterns. Thus the bottom-pair relation has respectively two and three unordered terms. This observation will allow coefficients on specified color counts to be detected later.
  - Explanation: Summing a bottom-pair bracket over the unordered frames of its plane gives zero, so every cycle functional obeys this local relation.
- **claim-ordinary-decorations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-decorations)): Let $M$ be a finite group, let $p$ be a prime, and let $h\geq2$ and $t\geq0$. Consider a finite nonempty set $\mathcal X$ of data $$(\mathcal F,\delta),\qquad \delta=(D_1,\ldots,D_t),$$ where $S_{\mathcal F}\leq M$ is a projective sign group of rank $r=h-1$, the labelled $D_i\leq M$ have order $p$, and $$E_{\mathcal F,\delta} =S_{\mathcal F}\times D_1\times\cdots\times D_t$$ is an internal direct product. Suppose the following hold.* 1. *The local coefficient space $V_{\mathcal F}$ satisfies <a href="#eq:chain-local-dimension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-local-dimension">[eq:chain-local-dimension]</a> and is the same whenever the same frame occurs with different decorations. For each fixed tuple $\delta$, its compatible frames form a set $\mathfrak F_\delta$ with a subspace $Z_\delta$ satisfying <a href="#eq:chain-frame-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-frame-input">[eq:chain-frame-input]</a>, with a common real number $F>0$. The boundary equality remains valid in the subgroup poset of $M$.* 2. *Put $\mathcal Z=\bigoplus_\delta Z_\delta$. The linear map $$\label{eq:chain-detection-map} \Phi:\mathcal Z\longrightarrow \widetilde C_{r+t-1}(\mathcal A_p(M)),\qquad \Phi(x)=\sum_{(\mathcal F,\delta)\in\mathcal X} x_{\mathcal F,\delta}\boxtimes a(\delta)$$ is injective.* 3. *For every $i\in\{1,\ldots,t\}$, each partial datum $(\mathcal F,D_1,\ldots,\widehat D_i,\ldots,D_t)$ that occurs has at least $k_i>0$ completions in $\mathcal X$.* *Write $N=\|\mathcal X\|$. Then the image of $\Phi$ contains a space of cycles of dimension at least $$\label{eq:chain-decoration-dimension} Nq_h\left(F-\sum_{i=1}^t\frac1{k_i}\right).$$ In particular, if $F>\sum_i1/k_i$, there is a nonzero cycle of degree $r+t-1$, supported on full flags ending at the rank-$r+t$ groups $E_{\mathcal F,\delta}$. At least one such full flag has a nonzero actual coefficient. When $t=0$, the sum is zero and there are no completion conditions.
  - Explanation: Adjoining commuting order-p axes and imposing one linear condition per partial datum leaves a cycle space whose dimension is bounded below when the frame fraction beats the reciprocal completion counts.
- **claim-quaternion-decoration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quaternion-decoration)): Let $M$ be a finite group, $h\geq2$, $r=h-1$, and $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}$. Consider a finite nonempty set $\mathcal X$ of data $(\mathcal F,T)$ if $\varepsilon=0$, or $(\mathcal F,T,B)$ if $\varepsilon=1$, where $$E=S_{\mathcal F}\times T\times B, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}S_{\mathcal F}=r,\quad T\cong C_2^2, \quad \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}B=\varepsilon.$$ For $\varepsilon=0$, set $B=1$ and omit it from the data. For each fixed $(T,B)$, assume its compatible frames have a space $Z_{T,B}$ satisfying <a href="#eq:chain-frame-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-frame-input">[eq:chain-frame-input]</a>, with a uniform lower bound $F>0$ and local bound $q_h=(h-1)!$. Assume that the map $$\label{eq:chain-quaternion-detection} \bigoplus_{(T,B)} Z_{T,B}\otimes Q_T \longrightarrow \widetilde C_{r+1+\varepsilon} (\mathcal A_2(M))$$ which sends a simple tensor to the sum of the split chains $x_{\mathcal F,T,B}\boxtimes q\boxtimes[B]$ is injective. When $B=1$, replace $[B]$ here by the empty simplex.* *For every occurring partial datum consisting of a frame, a retained line $\ell<T$, and $B$ when present, assume there are at least $k_T>0$ completions to full data. If $\varepsilon=1$, assume also that every occurring $(\mathcal F,T)$ has at least $k_B>0$ completions to a datum $(\mathcal F,T,B)$. Then the image in <a href="#eq:chain-quaternion-detection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chain-quaternion-detection">[eq:chain-quaternion-detection]</a> contains a cycle space of dimension at least $$\label{eq:chain-quaternion-dimension} 2\|\mathcal X\|q_h \left(F-\frac{3}{2k_T}-\frac{\varepsilon}{k_B}\right),$$ where the last term is omitted if $\varepsilon=0$. If the expression in parentheses is positive, there is a nonzero cycle on full flags ending at groups of rank $r+2+\varepsilon=h+1+\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: Decorating frame cycles with a rank-two group and its two-dimensional coefficient space gives a cycle space with an explicit lower dimension bound.
- **claim-opposite-chamber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-opposite-chamber)): Let $V$ be an $m$-dimensional vector space over $\mathbb F_p$, with $m\geq1$, and let $\mathcal B(V)$ be its poset of proper nonzero subspaces. Let $$0=F_0<F_1<\cdots<F_m=V,\qquad \dim F_j=j,$$ be a fixed full flag with its endpoints adjoined. Every nonzero cycle $z\in\widetilde C_{m-2}(\mathcal B(V))$ has a supported chamber $W_1<\cdots<W_{m-1}$ such that, on putting $W_0=0$ and $W_m=V$, $$\label{eq:chain-opposition} \dim(W_i\cap F_j)=\max\{0,i+j-m\} \quad(0\leq i,j\leq m).$$ In particular, if $0<c<m$ and $F_{m-c}=K$, then $W_c$ is a complement to $K$ in $V$.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero top cycle of the building has a supported chamber opposite to a fixed full flag, giving complements to prescribed subspaces.
- **claim-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction)): Let $M$ be a finite group, $H\leq M$, and $p$ a prime. Let $m\geq1$ and let $\alpha\in\widetilde C_{m-1}(\mathcal A_p(M))$ be a cycle supported on full flags ending at rank-$m$ elementary abelian groups. Denote by $\mathcal E(\alpha)$ the set of top groups of flags with nonzero coefficient in $\alpha$. Suppose that an integer $c$ satisfies $$0\leq c<m, \qquad [E':E'\cap H]\leq p^c \quad(E'\in\mathcal E(\alpha)),$$ and that a specified $E\in\mathcal E(\alpha)$ satisfies $[E:E\cap H]=p^c$. Then there is a nonzero cycle $$\beta\in\widetilde C_{m-c-1}(\mathcal A_p(H))$$ supported on full flags of rank $m-c$, with a nonzero coefficient at a full flag ending at $E\cap H$. If $m-c=m_p(H)$, this cycle represents a nonzero homology class.
  - Explanation: A homogeneous cycle with bounded index of top groups in a subgroup restricts to a nonzero cycle of the subgroup poset keeping a nonzero full-flag coefficient.
- **claim-projective-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projective-rank)): Let $D$ be an elementary abelian $p$-subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{PGL}}(V)$, where $\dim V=n$ and the characteristic of the field is different from $p$. The scalar commutators of representatives define an alternating bilinear pairing on $D$ with values in the $p$th roots of unity. If this pairing has rank $2a$, then $$p^a\mid n, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}D\le 2a+\frac{n}{p^a}-1.$$ In particular, for odd $p$, $n\ge5$, and $a>0$, $$\label{eq:odd-pairing-strict} \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}D<n-2.$$
  - Explanation: The commutator pairing of an elementary abelian projective subgroup bounds its rank, strictly below the dimension minus two at odd primes when the pairing is nonzero.
- **claim-linear-sign-centralizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-sign-centralizer)): Suppose $q$ is odd and $n\ge5$. Set $S=S(\mathcal D)$ and $S_L=S\cap L$ for a line frame as above, using $p=2$. Every automorphism centralizing $S_L$ preserves each line of $\mathcal D$, or matches it with the corresponding line of the dual frame when it is a correlation. It centralizes $S$. Every involution in $M$ centralizing $S_L$ belongs to $S$.
  - Explanation: Automorphisms centralizing the inner sign subgroup preserve each frame line or match it with the dual frame.
- **claim-linear-unitary-two** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-linear-two)): Let $G$ have least order among finite groups with $O_2(G)=1$ and $\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_2(G);\mathbb Q)=0$. Then $G$ has no simple component isomorphic to $\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_n(q)$ or $\mathop{\mathrm{PSU}}_n(q)$ with $n\ge5$ and $q$ of characteristic at least five.
  - Explanation: A minimal counterexample at two has no linear or unitary simple component of natural dimension at least five in characteristic at least five.
- **claim-unitary-odd** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-unitary-odd)): Let $p$ be an odd prime, let $q$ be odd with $p\mid q+1$, and let $L=\mathop{\mathrm{PSU}}_n(q)$ with $n\ge5$. If $L\le H\le\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ and $H/L$ is elementary abelian of $p$-power order, then $$\widetilde H_{m_p(H)-1}(\mathcal A_p(H);\mathbb Q)\ne0.$$ Here $m_p(H)$ denotes the maximum rank of an elementary abelian $p$-subgroup of $H$.
  - Explanation: For the indicated unitary extensions at odd primes, homology in the largest possible degree is nonzero.
- **claim-symplectic-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-symplectic)): A minimal-order counterexample to <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a> at $p=2$ cannot have a simple component isomorphic to $\mathop{\mathrm{PSp}}_{2n}(q)$, where $n\geq3$ and $q$ has characteristic at least five.
  - Explanation: A minimal counterexample at two has no symplectic simple component of the listed type.
- **claim-symp-centralizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-symp-centralizer)): For $n\geq3$ and a pair $(\mathcal D,T)$ as above, $$C_M\bigl(A_0(\mathcal D,T)\bigr)=A_0(\mathcal D,T).$$ Every semilinear projective transformation centralizing $S(\mathcal D)$ preserves each plane of $\mathcal D$ individually.
  - Explanation: The plane-and-quaternion seed is self-centralizing in the similitude group, and semilinear centralizers of the signs fix each plane.
- **claim-symp-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-symp-descent)): Let $T=\langle\overline I,\overline J\rangle$ satisfy <a href="#eq:symp-quaternion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:symp-quaternion">[eq:symp-quaternion]</a>, and let the projective semilinear involution $b$ centralize $S(\mathcal D)T$ and induce $\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(\mathbb F_q/\mathbb F_s)$, where $q=s^2$. Replacing $b$ by $tb$ for some $t\in T$ leaves $\langle S(\mathcal D),T,b\rangle$ unchanged and permits a semilinear representative $B$ with $$B^2=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_V,\qquad BI=IB,\qquad BJ=JB.$$ After scaling $\omega$, its restriction to $V_0=\{v:Bv=v\}$ is a nondegenerate alternating form over $\mathbb F_s$, and $I,J$ descend to symplectic operators on $V_0$. For fixed $T,b$ satisfying these exact commutation conditions, the compatible plane decompositions are all orthogonal nondegenerate line frames of a symmetric $n$-dimensional space over $\mathbb F_s$.
  - Explanation: A field involution can be normalized to commute exactly with the quaternion lifts, and the form descends to a symmetric multiplicity space over the subfield.
- **claim-symp-completions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-symp-completions)): Let $u$ be as in <a href="#eq:symp-field-parameter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:symp-field-parameter">[eq:symp-field-parameter]</a>, and let $\chi_u$ denote the quadratic character of $\mathbb F_u^\times$. A fixed plane frame and retained line of a compatible quaternion four-group, together with a fixed admissible field axis when present, have at least $$k_T=\frac{(u-\chi_u(-1))^n}{4}\geq4^{n-1}$$ completions to admissible full data. In the field case, every retained pair $(\mathcal D,T)$ that occurs has at least $k_b=2^{n-1}$ admissible field-axis completions in $H\setminus L$.
  - Explanation: Restoring a quaternion four-group from a retained line, and a field axis, have large completion counts growing with the number of planes.
- **claim-orthogonal-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-orthogonal)): Let $G$ be a minimal-order counterexample to rational augmented reduced homology nonvanishing at the prime two. Thus $O_2(G)=1$ and $\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_2(G);\mathbb Q)=0$, whereas the asserted nonvanishing holds for smaller groups with trivial $2$-core. Then $G$ has no simple component $L=\mathrm P\Omega(V)$, where $V$ is a nondegenerate symmetric space over $\mathbb F_q$, the characteristic of $\mathbb F_q$ is at least five, and its dimension $N$ is odd with $N\ge5$, even with $N\ge8$, or equal to six, except possibly when $N=6$, $q=5$, and the determinant of the form is a square. In odd dimension $\mathrm P\Omega(V)=\Omega(V)$.
  - Explanation: A minimal counterexample at two has no orthogonal simple component in the stated range except possibly the square-determinant six-dimensional case over the smallest allowed field.
- **claim-orthogonal-centralizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonal-centralizer)): Let $T=T(\mathcal D)$ distinguish all $N$ positions and satisfy $\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}T>N/2$. Then every natural semilinear similarity centralizing $T$ projectively preserves each line of $\mathcal D$. Its linear centralizer is exactly $S(\mathcal D)$. In dimension eight the same conclusions hold for the full automorphism centralizer if some color class has at least three members. Every elementary abelian subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ containing $T$ therefore lies in the natural semilinear group, centralizes $S(\mathcal D)$, and has at most one direction beyond its subgroup in $S(\mathcal D)$.
  - Explanation: A separating inner sign subgroup of rank above half the dimension forces its centralizer to preserve the frame lines and allows at most one outer direction.
- **claim-orthogonal-eight-naturality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonal-eight-naturality)): Let $V$ be a nondegenerate symmetric space of dimension eight over $\mathbb F_q$, with defining characteristic at least five, and put $L=\mathrm P\Omega(V)$. If $V$ has minus type, every automorphism of $L$ is induced by a natural semilinear similarity of $V$.* *If $V$ has plus type, let $\ell_1,\ell_2,\ell_3$ be mutually orthogonal nondegenerate lines of the same norm square class. Let $x,y$ be the projective isometries changing the signs on $\ell_1\oplus\ell_2$ and $\ell_2\oplus\ell_3$, respectively, and fixing their orthogonal complements. Then $x,y\in L$, and every automorphism of $L$ centralizing both is induced by a natural semilinear similarity of $V$.
  - Explanation: In dimension eight automorphisms are natural in minus type, and in plus type those centralizing two chosen sign flips are natural.
- **claim-orthogonal-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonal-descent)): Suppose an involution $b$ of the natural projective semilinear group has nontrivial field action and preserves every line of a nondegenerate orthogonal frame of $V$. Then $q=s^2$, and after scalar normalization a representative of $b$ is an involutory semilinear map $B$ with fixed space $V_0$ over $\mathbb F_s$. A scalar multiple of the form descends to a nondegenerate symmetric form on $V_0$. The compatible frames are exactly the scalar extensions of its nondegenerate orthogonal frames. All their lines have one common norm square class over $\mathbb F_q$. In particular, when $N$ is even the determinant over $\mathbb F_q$ is square.
  - Explanation: A field involution preserving an orthogonal frame forces a square field order and descent to a symmetric space over the subfield with one common square class.
- **claim-orthogonal-five-colors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orthogonal-five-colors)): Let $V$ be a square-determinant symmetric space over $\mathbb F_5$, of odd dimension $N\ge5$ or even dimension $N\ge8$. The space of actual sign cycle coefficients produced by Theorem <a href="#thm:frame-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:frame-bound">11</a> contains an assignment with a nonzero full-flag coefficient at an admissible frame as defined above.
  - Explanation: Over the smallest allowed field the produced cycle coefficients cannot vanish on all admissible colour patterns.
- **claim-psl45-intermediate-core** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-psl45-intermediate-core)): Let $J\trianglelefteq H\leq G$ be finite groups, and suppose that $H/J$ is a $p$-group. If $O_p(C_G(H))=1$, then $O_p(C_G(J))=1$.
  - Explanation: Trivial p-core of the centralizer of a p-group extension implies trivial p-core of the centralizer of the normal subgroup.
- **claim-psl45-homology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-psl45-homology)): For $L=\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_4(5)$, $$\widetilde\chi(\mathcal A_2(L))\equiv-1\pmod5.$$ In particular $\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_2(L);\mathbb Q)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The reduced Euler characteristic of the 2-subgroup poset of the component is congruent to minus one modulo five, so its rational homology is nonzero.
- **claim-psl45-equivalent-poset** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-psl45-equivalent-poset)): Let $G$ be a finite group, let $p$ be a prime, and let $L$ be a centerless component of $G$ with $p\mid \|L\|$. Put $K=C_G(L)$ and $$\mathcal Q= \bigl((\mathcal A_p(L)\cup\{1\})\times (\mathcal A_p(K)\cup\{1\})\bigr)\setminus\{(1,1)\}, \qquad \mathcal F=\{F\in\mathcal A_p(G):F\cap LK=1\}.$$ Give $\mathcal Q$ the coordinatewise order and $\mathcal F$ the inclusion order. Define $$\mathcal F_0= \{F\in\mathcal F:F\leq N_G(L),\ O_p(C_G(LF))\ne1\}.$$ The disjoint union $\mathcal X=\mathcal Q\sqcup\mathcal F$ is ordered by these orders and by the following crossed comparisons: $$\label{eq:psl45-cross} F<(U,V) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \begin{cases} C_{UV}(F)\ne1,\\ C_{UV}(F)\not\leq L&\text{if }F\in\mathcal F_0. \end{cases}$$ There are no comparisons directed from $\mathcal Q$ to $\mathcal F$. These rules define a poset, and $\mathcal X\simeq\mathcal A_p(G)$.
  - Explanation: For a centerless component, the subgroup poset is homotopy equivalent to a poset assembled from a product part and a faithful-complement part with crossed comparisons.
- **claim-psl45-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-psl45)): A minimal-order counterexample to the rational homological implication at $p=2$ has no simple component isomorphic to $\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_4(5)$.
  - Explanation: A minimal counterexample at two has no simple component of this type, by three branches.

          "exact_quote": "Assume the inductive hypothesis on proper subgroups of $G$ in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:propagation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:propagation\">9</a>, and let $L$ be a nonabelian simple component with $p\\mid|L|$. Let $\\mathcal F$ be a nonempty family of faithful configurations for $L$ in $G$ such that $$A\\in\\mathcal F,\\quad D\\in\\mathcal A_p(G),\\quad D>A,\n \\quad D\\text{ a faithful configuration}\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad D\\in\\mathcal F.$$ Let $r$ be the largest rank of a member of $\\mathcal F$. If some $A\\in\\mathcal F$ of rank $r$ is the final subgroup of a saturated flag having nonzero coefficient in a cycle of degree $r-1$ in $\\Delta\\mathcal A_p(LA)$, then $\\widetilde H_*(\\Delta\\mathcal A_p(G);\\mathbb Q)\\ne0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-310.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Assume the inductive hypothesis on proper subgroups of $G$ in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:propagation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:propagation\">9</a>, and let $L$ be a nonabelian simple component with $p\\mid|L|$. If some $A\\in\\mathcal F$ of rank $r$ is the final subgroup of a saturated flag having nonzero coefficient in a cycle of degree $r-1$ in $\\Delta\\mathcal A_p(LA)$, then $\\widetilde H_*(\\Delta\\mathcal A_p(G);\\mathbb Q)\\ne0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-frame-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ have dimension $h\\geq2$ and one of the four structures above, and suppose that $W$ admits an allowed frame. The space $\\mathcal Z(W)\\subseteq\\bigoplus_{\\mathcal D\\in\\mathfrak F(W)}V_{\\mathcal D}$ consists of cycles of degree $h-2$, each supported on full flags ending at sign groups of rank $h-1$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:frame-bound}\n \\dim\\mathcal Z(W)\\ \\geq\\\n F_h\\,|\\mathfrak F(W)|(h-1)!.$$ The following choices of $F_h$ are valid for each individual isometry type of $W$.*\n\n1.  *In the no-form case over $\\mathbb F_u$, and in the hermitian case over $\\mathbb F_{u^2}$, set $$\\rho=\\frac1{u(u+1)}\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n     \\rho=\\frac1{u(u-1)},\n     \\quad\\hbox{respectively},\\qquad\n     y=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4\\rho}}2.$$ For $u\\geq5$ one may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h\\geq(17/25)^h$.*\n\n\n2.  *For a symmetric space over $\\mathbb F_u$, with both line types allowed and $u\\geq5$ odd, put $$x=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4u/(u^2-1)}}2.$$ One may take $$\\label{eq:symmetric-frame-fraction}\n     F_h=x^h+(1-x)^h-\\mathbf1_{2\\mid h}\\,2(u^2-1)^{-h/2}\n     \\ \\geq\\ (17/25)^h.$$ In particular $F_3\\geq3/8$.*\n\n3.  *For a symmetric space over $\\mathbb F_u$ with one fixed line type, where $u\\geq7$ is odd, write $\\chi$ for the quadratic character of $\\mathbb F_u^\\times$ and set $$\\rho=\\frac2{u-\\chi(-1)},\\qquad\n     y=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4\\rho}}2.$$ One may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h>0$.*\n\n*The same statements hold for any realization by projective sign subgroups in which coarsened decompositions give the indicated subgroup flags and the complete flags ending at the full frames remain distinct.",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-frame-bound",

Moreover, $$\\label{eq:frame-bound}\n \\dim\\mathcal Z(W)\\ \\geq\\\n F_h\\,|\\mathfrak F(W)|(h-1)!.$$ The following choices of $F_h$ are valid for each individual isometry type of $W$.*\n\n1.  *In the no-form case over $\\mathbb F_u$, and in the hermitian case over $\\mathbb F_{u^2}$, set $$\\rho=\\frac1{u(u+1)}\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n     \\rho=\\frac1{u(u-1)},\n     \\quad\\hbox{respectively},\\qquad\n     y=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4\\rho}}2.$$ For $u\\geq5$ one may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h\\geq(17/25)^h$.*\n\n\n2.  *For a symmetric space over $\\mathbb F_u$, with both line types allowed and $u\\geq5$ odd, put $$x=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4u/(u^2-1)}}2.$$ One may take $$\\label{eq:symmetric-frame-fraction}\n     F_h=x^h+(1-x)^h-\\mathbf1_{2\\mid h}\\,2(u^2-1)^{-h/2}\n     \\ \\geq\\ (17/25)^h.$$ In particular $F_3\\geq3/8$.*\n\n3.  *For a symmetric space over $\\mathbb F_u$ with one fixed line type, where $u\\geq7$ is odd, write $\\chi$ for the quadratic character of $\\mathbb F_u^\\times$ and set $$\\rho=\\frac2{u-\\chi(-1)},\\qquad\n     y=\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-4\\rho}}2.$$ One may take $F_h=y^h+(1-y)^h>0$.*\n\n*The same statements hold for any realization by projective sign subgroups in which coarsened decompositions give the indicated subgroup flags and the complete flags ending at the full frames remain distinct.",
          "source_id": "PM-310.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-merger-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "For a full flag in $\\mathcal D(W)$ ending at a frame $\\mathcal D$, complete its sequence of binary mergers by the final, implicit merger to the one-block partition. Its coefficient in $\\sum_w f(w)a_w$ is, up to one sign depending on the flag and the chosen child orders, the evaluation of $f$ on the associated iterated superbracket. All iterated brackets on the letters of $\\mathcal D$ occur in this way. Their span has dimension $(h-1)!$.",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-merger-coefficients",
          "exact_quote": "For a full flag in $\\mathcal D(W)$ ending at a frame $\\mathcal D$, complete its sequence of binary mergers by the final, implicit merger to the one-block partition.           "source_id": "PM-310.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rem-frame-cherry",
        "exact_quote": "The coefficient description gives a useful relation at every bottom pair of a merger tree. Fix all other leaves and the surrounding brackets. Replacing the bracket $[e_A,e_B]$ by the sum of these brackets over the unordered frames of the same plane gives zero in $P$, by <a href=\"#eq:frame-rq\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:frame-rq\">[eq:frame-rq]</a>. Every functional representing a member of $\\mathcal Z(W)$ therefore satisfies this relation on the actual tree coefficients. For example, when $u=5$, a plane with square determinant has exactly one unordered frame of each homogeneous color, and a plane with nonsquare determinant has three unordered mixed frames. These counts follow from <a href=\"#eq:orthogonal-plane-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:orthogonal-plane-order\">[eq:orthogonal-plane-order]</a>: the respective group orders are $8$ and $12$, and an ordered line-frame stabilizer has order $4$. For a homogeneous color one divides its two ordered frames by $2$; for a mixed frame the two possible orders have different color patterns. Thus the bottom-pair relation has respectively two and three unordered terms. This observation will allow coefficients on specified color counts to be detected later.",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rem-frame-cherry",
          "exact_quote": "The coefficient description gives a useful relation at every bottom pair of a merger tree. Fix all other leaves and the surrounding brackets. Every functional representing a member of $\\mathcal Z(W)$ therefore satisfies this relation on the actual tree coefficients. For example, when $u=5$, a plane with square determinant has exactly one unordered frame of each homogeneous color, and a plane with nonsquare determinant has three unordered mixed frames. These counts follow from <a href=\"#eq:orthogonal-plane-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:orthogonal-plane-order\">[eq:orthogonal-plane-order]</a>: the respective group orders are $8$ and $12$, and an ordered line-frame stabilizer has order $4$. For a homogeneous color one divides its two ordered frames by $2$; for a mixed frame the two possible orders have different color patterns. Thus the bottom-pair relation has respectively two and three unordered terms. This observation will allow coefficients on specified color counts to be detected later.",
          "source_id": "PM-310.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-decorations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a finite group, let $p$ be a prime, and let $h\\geq2$ and $t\\geq0$. Consider a finite nonempty set $\\mathcal X$ of data $$(\\mathcal F,\\delta),\\qquad\n \\delta=(D_1,\\ldots,D_t),$$ where $S_{\\mathcal F}\\leq M$ is a projective sign group of rank $r=h-1$, the labelled $D_i\\leq M$ have order $p$, and $$E_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}\n   =S_{\\mathcal F}\\times D_1\\times\\cdots\\times D_t$$ is an internal direct product. Suppose the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The local coefficient space $V_{\\mathcal F}$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:chain-local-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chain-local-dimension\">[eq:chain-local-dimension]</a> and is the same whenever the same frame occurs with different decorations. For each fixed tuple $\\delta$, its compatible frames form a set $\\mathfrak F_\\delta$ with a subspace $Z_\\delta$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:chain-frame-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chain-frame-input\">[eq:chain-frame-input]</a>, with a common real number $F>0$. The boundary equality remains valid in the subgroup poset of $M$.*\n\n\n2.  *Put $\\mathcal Z=\\bigoplus_\\delta Z_\\delta$. The linear map $$\\label{eq:chain-detection-map}\n     \\Phi:\\mathcal Z\\longrightarrow\n           \\widetilde C_{r+t-1}(\\mathcal A_p(M)),\\qquad\n     \\Phi(x)=\\sum_{(\\mathcal F,\\delta)\\in\\mathcal X}\n     x_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}\\boxtimes a(\\delta)$$ is injective.*\n\n3.  *For every $i\\in\\{1,\\ldots,t\\}$, each partial datum $(\\mathcal F,D_1,\\ldots,\\widehat D_i,\\ldots,D_t)$ that occurs has at least $k_i>0$ completions in $\\mathcal X$.*\n\n\n*Write $N=|\\mathcal X|$. Then the image of $\\Phi$ contains a space of cycles of dimension at least $$\\label{eq:chain-decoration-dimension}\n Nq_h\\left(F-\\sum_{i=1}^t\\frac1{k_i}\\right).$$ In particular, if $F>\\sum_i1/k_i$, there is a nonzero cycle of degree $r+t-1$, supported on full flags ending at the rank-$r+t$ groups $E_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}$. At least one such full flag has a nonzero actual coefficient. When $t=0$, the sum is zero and there are no completion conditions.",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-decorations",

Suppose the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The local coefficient space $V_{\\mathcal F}$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:chain-local-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chain-local-dimension\">[eq:chain-local-dimension]</a> and is the same whenever the same frame occurs with different decorations. The boundary equality remains valid in the subgroup poset of $M$.*\n\n\n2.  *Put $\\mathcal Z=\\bigoplus_\\delta Z_\\delta$. The linear map $$\\label{eq:chain-detection-map}\n     \\Phi:\\mathcal Z\\longrightarrow\n           \\widetilde C_{r+t-1}(\\mathcal A_p(M)),\\qquad\n     \\Phi(x)=\\sum_{(\\mathcal F,\\delta)\\in\\mathcal X}\n     x_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}\\boxtimes a(\\delta)$$ is injective.*\n\n3.  *For every $i\\in\\{1,\\ldots,t\\}$, each partial datum $(\\mathcal F,D_1,\\ldots,\\widehat D_i,\\ldots,D_t)$ that occurs has at least $k_i>0$ completions in $\\mathcal X$.*\n\n\n*Write $N=|\\mathcal X|$. Then the image of $\\Phi$ contains a space of cycles of dimension at least $$\\label{eq:chain-decoration-dimension}\n Nq_h\\left(F-\\sum_{i=1}^t\\frac1{k_i}\\right).$$ In particular, if $F>\\sum_i1/k_i$, there is a nonzero cycle of degree $r+t-1$, supported on full flags ending at the rank-$r+t$ groups $E_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}$. Then the image of $\\Phi$ contains a space of cycles of dimension at least $$\\label{eq:chain-decoration-dimension}\n Nq_h\\left(F-\\sum_{i=1}^t\\frac1{k_i}\\right).$$ In particular, if $F>\\sum_i1/k_i$, there is a nonzero cycle of degree $r+t-1$, supported on full flags ending at the rank-$r+t$ groups $E_{\\mathcal F,\\delta}$.         "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a finite group, $h\\geq2$, $r=h-1$, and $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}$. Consider a finite nonempty set $\\mathcal X$ of data $(\\mathcal F,T)$ if $\\varepsilon=0$, or $(\\mathcal F,T,B)$ if $\\varepsilon=1$, where $$E=S_{\\mathcal F}\\times T\\times B,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}S_{\\mathcal F}=r,\\quad T\\cong C_2^2,\n \\quad \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}B=\\varepsilon.$$ For $\\varepsilon=0$, set $B=1$ and omit it from the data. For each fixed $(T,B)$, assume its compatible frames have a space $Z_{T,B}$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:chain-frame-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chain-frame-input\">[eq:chain-frame-input]</a>, with a uniform lower bound $F>0$ and local bound $q_h=(h-1)!$. Assume that the map $$\\label{eq:chain-quaternion-detection}\n \\bigoplus_{(T,B)} Z_{T,B}\\otimes Q_T\n \\longrightarrow \\widetilde C_{r+1+\\varepsilon}\n                       (\\mathcal A_2(M))$$ which sends a simple tensor to the sum of the split chains $x_{\\mathcal F,T,B}\\boxtimes q\\boxtimes[B]$ is injective. When $B=1$, replace $[B]$ here by the empty simplex.*\n\n\n*For every occurring partial datum consisting of a frame, a retained line $\\ell<T$, and $B$ when present, assume there are at least $k_T>0$ completions to full data. If $\\varepsilon=1$, assume also that every occurring $(\\mathcal F,T)$ has at least $k_B>0$ completions to a datum $(\\mathcal F,T,B)$. Then the image in <a href=\"#eq:chain-quaternion-detection\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chain-quaternion-detection\">[eq:chain-quaternion-detection]</a> contains a cycle space of dimension at least $$\\label{eq:chain-quaternion-dimension}\n 2|\\mathcal X|q_h\n \\left(F-\\frac{3}{2k_T}-\\frac{\\varepsilon}{k_B}\\right),$$ where the last term is omitted if $\\varepsilon=0$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-opposite-chamber",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be an $m$-dimensional vector space over $\\mathbb F_p$, with $m\\geq1$, and let $\\mathcal B(V)$ be its poset of proper nonzero subspaces. Let $$0=F_0<F_1<\\cdots<F_m=V,\\qquad \\dim F_j=j,$$ be a fixed full flag with its endpoints adjoined. Every nonzero cycle $z\\in\\widetilde C_{m-2}(\\mathcal B(V))$ has a supported chamber $W_1<\\cdots<W_{m-1}$ such that, on putting $W_0=0$ and $W_m=V$, $$\\label{eq:chain-opposition}\n \\dim(W_i\\cap F_j)=\\max\\{0,i+j-m\\}\n       \\quad(0\\leq i,j\\leq m).$$ In particular, if $0<c<m$ and $F_{m-c}=K$, then $W_c$ is a complement to $K$ in $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a finite group, $H\\leq M$, and $p$ a prime. Let $m\\geq1$ and let $\\alpha\\in\\widetilde C_{m-1}(\\mathcal A_p(M))$ be a cycle supported on full flags ending at rank-$m$ elementary abelian groups. Denote by $\\mathcal E(\\alpha)$ the set of top groups of flags with nonzero coefficient in $\\alpha$. Suppose that an integer $c$ satisfies $$0\\leq c<m,\n \\qquad [E':E'\\cap H]\\leq p^c\n      \\quad(E'\\in\\mathcal E(\\alpha)),$$ and that a specified $E\\in\\mathcal E(\\alpha)$ satisfies $[E:E\\cap H]=p^c$. Then there is a nonzero cycle $$\\beta\\in\\widetilde C_{m-c-1}(\\mathcal A_p(H))$$ supported on full flags of rank $m-c$, with a nonzero coefficient at a full flag ending at $E\\cap H$. If $m-c=m_p(H)$, this cycle represents a nonzero homology class.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-projective-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be an elementary abelian $p$-subgroup of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}(V)$, where $\\dim V=n$ and the characteristic of the field is different from $p$. The scalar commutators of representatives define an alternating bilinear pairing on $D$ with values in the $p$th roots of unity. If this pairing has rank $2a$, then $$p^a\\mid n,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D\\le 2a+\\frac{n}{p^a}-1.$$ In particular, for odd $p$, $n\\ge5$, and $a>0$, $$\\label{eq:odd-pairing-strict}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D<n-2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-310.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-projective-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be an elementary abelian $p$-subgroup of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}(V)$, where $\\dim V=n$ and the characteristic of the field is different from $p$. The scalar commutators of representatives define an alternating bilinear pairing on $D$ with values in the $p$th roots of unity. If this pairing has rank $2a$, then $$p^a\\mid n,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D\\le 2a+\\frac{n}{p^a}-1.$$ In particular, for odd $p$, $n\\ge5$, and $a>0$, $$\\label{eq:odd-pairing-strict}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D<n-2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-310.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $D$ be an elementary abelian $p$-subgroup of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PGL}}(V)$, where $\\dim V=n$ and the characteristic of the field is different from $p$. If this pairing has rank $2a$, then $$p^a\\mid n,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D\\le 2a+\\frac{n}{p^a}-1.$$ In particular, for odd $p$, $n\\ge5$, and $a>0$, $$\\label{eq:odd-pairing-strict}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}D<n-2.$$",
      "explanation": "The commutator pairing of an elementary abelian projective subgroup bounds its rank, strictly below the dimension minus two at odd primes when the pairing is nonzero.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $T=\\langle\\overline I,\\overline J\\rangle$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:symp-quaternion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symp-quaternion\">[eq:symp-quaternion]</a>, and let the projective semilinear involution $b$ centralize $S(\\mathcal D)T$ and induce $\\sigma\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gal}}(\\mathbb F_q/\\mathbb F_s)$, where $q=s^2$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-symp-completions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u$ be as in <a href=\"#eq:symp-field-parameter\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:symp-field-parameter\">[eq:symp-field-parameter]</a>, and let $\\chi_u$ denote the quadratic character of $\\mathbb F_u^\\times$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-orthogonal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a minimal-order counterexample to rational augmented reduced homology nonvanishing at the prime two. Thus $O_2(G)=1$ and $\\widetilde H_*(\\mathcal A_2(G);\\mathbb Q)=0$, whereas the asserted nonvanishing holds for smaller groups with trivial $2$-core. Then $G$ has no simple component $L=\\mathrm P\\Omega(V)$, where $V$ is a nondegenerate symmetric space over $\\mathbb F_q$, the characteristic of $\\mathbb F_q$ is at least five, and its dimension $N$ is odd with $N\\ge5$, even with $N\\ge8$, or equal to six, except possibly when $N=6$, $q=5$, and the determinant of the form is a square.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-orthogonal-centralizer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T=T(\\mathcal D)$ distinguish all $N$ positions and satisfy $\\mathop{\\mathrm{rk}}T>N/2$. Then every natural semilinear similarity centralizing $T$ projectively preserves each line of $\\mathcal D$. Its linear centralizer is exactly $S(\\mathcal D)$. In dimension eight the same conclusions hold for the full automorphism centralizer if some color class has at least three members. Every elementary abelian subgroup of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ containing $T$ therefore lies in the natural semilinear group, centralizes $S(\\mathcal D)$, and has at most one direction beyond its subgroup in $S(\\mathcal D)$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a nondegenerate symmetric space of dimension eight over $\\mathbb F_q$, with defining characteristic at least five, and put $L=\\mathrm P\\Omega(V)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-orthogonal-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose an involution $b$ of the natural projective semilinear group has nontrivial field action and preserves every line of a nondegenerate orthogonal frame of $V$. Then $q=s^2$, and after scalar normalization a representative of $b$ is an involutory semilinear map $B$ with fixed space $V_0$ over $\\mathbb F_s$. A scalar multiple of the form descends to a nondegenerate symmetric form on $V_0$. The compatible frames are exactly the scalar extensions of its nondegenerate orthogonal frames. All their lines have one common norm square class over $\\mathbb F_q$. In particular, when $N$ is even the determinant over $\\mathbb F_q$ is square.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **elementary abelian subgroup poset** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The poset $\mathcal A_p(G)$ consists of its nonidentity elementary abelian $p$-subgroups, ordered by inclusion.
- **p-core** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $O_p(G)$ for the largest normal $p$-subgroup of $G$.
- **component** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A component of a finite group is a subnormal quasisimple subgroup.
- **elementary p-extension** ([source](#pm-h-9)): An *elementary $p$-extension* of $L$ means a split extension $L\rtimes B$ in which $B$ is elementary abelian and induces only outer automorphisms; $B=1$ is allowed.
- **faithful configuration** ([source](#stmt-def-faithful-configuration)): Fix a finite group $G$, a prime $p$, and a nonabelian simple component $L$ of $G$. A *faithful configuration for $L$ in $G$* is a subgroup $A\in\mathcal A_p(G)$ such that $$A\le N_G(L),\qquad A\cap L\ne1,\qquad C_{LA}(L)=1,\qquad O_p(C_G(LA))=1.$$ We write $H=LA$. The condition $C_H(L)=1$ says that the conjugation homomorphism $H\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ is injective. We may therefore identify $L\le H\le\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ using this homomorphism.
- **frame** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A *frame* of an admissible space $W$ is an unordered decomposition $\mathcal D=\{L_1,\ldots,L_h\}$ into allowed lines, where $h=\dim W$.
- **decomposition poset** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Let $\mathcal D(W)$ be the poset of decompositions of $W$ into at least two nonzero admissible subspaces.
- **correlation** ([source](#pm-h-29)): A *correlation* here is a linear or semilinear identification with the dual space, acting on the group through inverse transpose.
- **compatible frame** ([source](#subsec:linear-compatible)): A frame is compatible with it if every line is stable, with matching to the dual frame in the correlation case.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-core** [claim](#stmt-lem-component-core) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-component-core): Component theorem inside the centralizer and characteristic subgroup argument. -> trivial p-core of the centralizer
- **s-faithful** [claim](#stmt-lem-faithfulness-upgrade) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-faithfulness-upgrade): Trivial intersection of distinct conjugates and unique factorization. -> normalization and faithfulness of overgroups
- **s-residue** [claim](#stmt-lem-saturated-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-saturated-residue): Sign bookkeeping of deleted vertices after a saturated flag. -> residue anticommutes with boundary
- **s-shuffle** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-shuffle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-shuffle): Signed sum over shuffles with pairwise cancellation of faces. -> product chain operation
- **s-propagation** [claim](#stmt-lem-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-propagation): Delete unfaithful overgroups, multiply with a centralizer cycle, detect by residue. -> nonzero homology of the ambient group
- **s-maximal-family** [claim](#stmt-cor-maximal-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-maximal-family): Maximal rank contradiction for each violated propagation condition. -> propagation hypotheses
- **s-merger** [claim](#stmt-lem-merger-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-merger-coefficients): Sign count on interval orders and spanning by left combs. -> bracket description of coefficients
- **s-lie-ident** [claim](#stmt-lem-frame-lie-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frame-lie-identification): Annihilator of the defining ideal and PBW injectivity. -> cycle space dimension equals Lie component dimension
- **s-marked-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-frame-marked-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frame-marked-factor): Leading module monomials and weighted marks. -> marked-factor inequality
- **s-frame-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-frame-bound) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Logarithmic series comparison and plane counts. -> frame fraction bounds
- **s-cherry** [claim](#stmt-rem-frame-cherry) / [proof](#stmt-rem-frame-cherry): Plane relation at a bottom pair. -> local relation
- **s-decorations** [claim](#stmt-lem-decorations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-decorations): One linear condition per partial datum and rank counting. -> cycle space bound
- **s-quaternion** [claim](#stmt-lem-quaternion-decoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quaternion-decoration): Retained-line conditions on a rank-two decoration. -> cycle space bound
- **s-opposite** [claim](#stmt-lem-opposite-chamber) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite-chamber): Maximal inversion number of the relative position permutation. -> opposite supported chamber
- **s-restriction** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction): Residue at a complement flag and injective intersection. -> restricted nonzero cycle
- **s-projective-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-projective-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projective-rank): Radical weights and free translation orbits. -> rank bound
- **s-linear-centralizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-linear-sign-centralizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-linear-sign-centralizer): Affine rank of weights under translation. -> line-preserving centralizer
- **s-linear-two** [claim](#stmt-prop-linear-two) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linear-two): Decorated frame cycle, restriction, maximal family. -> exclusion of linear and unitary components
- **s-unitary-odd** [claim](#stmt-prop-unitary-odd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-unitary-odd): Rank dichotomy and a top-degree cycle. -> top-degree nonvanishing
- **s-symplectic** [claim](#stmt-prop-symplectic) / [proof](#pm-h-38): Quaternion-decorated cycle with completion counts and propagation. -> exclusion of symplectic components
- **s-orthogonal** [claim](#stmt-prop-orthogonal) / [proof](#pm-h-44): Colour cases, descent, and a contradiction on colour patterns. -> exclusion of orthogonal components
- **s-psl45-core** [claim](#stmt-lem-psl45-intermediate-core) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-psl45-intermediate-core): Orbit counting. -> inherited trivial core
- **s-psl45-homology** [claim](#stmt-lem-psl45-homology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-psl45-homology): Fixed-point-free Sylow 5 action and orbit counting. -> Euler characteristic congruence
- **s-psl45** [claim](#stmt-prop-psl45) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-psl45): Three-branch exclusion. -> exclusion of the component
- **s-reduction-odd** [claim](#stmt-thm-reduction-odd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-reduction-odd): Derivation from the cited odd-prime reduction. -> conditional odd-prime result
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-psl45-2): Minimal counterexample against the reduction list. -> main theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-nonvanishing-rational-homology**: For a finite group with trivial largest normal p-subgroup, the augmented reduced rational homology of the poset of nonidentity elementary abelian p-subgroups is nonzero in some degree; the empty poset counts through its degree minus one class.
- **inv-centralizer-core-inheritance**: If the ambient finite group has trivial p-core, then the centralizer of any nonabelian simple component also has trivial p-core, which lets the induction hypothesis be applied to that centralizer.
- **inv-restriction-keeps-coefficient**: A cycle on full flags of fixed rank in a finite group M, whose top groups have intersection index with a subgroup H bounded by p^c and attained at a chosen top group, restricts to a nonzero cycle in the poset of H keeping one nonzero full-flag coefficient.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-infinite-groups**: Apply the nonvanishing conclusion to infinite or topological groups. -> The statement and its proof rely on finiteness of the group and of its subgroup poset; nothing is asserted outside that setting.
- **anti-top-degree-at-two**: Assume the nonzero class always sits in the largest possible chain degree at the prime two. -> At the prime two the argument only produces nonvanishing in some degree through propagation, not the top-degree dimension property.
- **anti-nontrivial-core**: Expect nonzero homology of the elementary abelian poset when the p-core is nontrivial. -> A nontrivial normal p-subgroup makes the poset contractible, so reduced homology vanishes.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result and its supporting lemmas are existential or structural statements about homology of the elementary abelian subgroup poset of arbitrary finite groups, proved by contradiction from a minimal counterexample and by cycle constructions whose coefficient spaces are only bounded in dimension; the paper gives no finite procedure that outputs an object, so no executable reference implementation or independent oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper states an existence and nonvanishing theorem about homology of a subgroup poset. It gives no program, interface or data format that a software library could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are frames, sign spaces and chains over finite fields and finite groups. No dynamical system, state space or simulation step appears in the source. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The augmented chain convention is a proof device. The paper offers no tool, computation scheme or checker built on it. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The propagation lemma transfers a nonbounding cycle only after one is assumed or constructed by dimension counting. Dimension bounds guarantee nonzero cycles, but no finite procedure to find them or to bound its cost is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The identification of the cycle space dimension with the Lie quotient dimension is a counting statement. It does not define a representation or index with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic kernel, memory layout or parallel structure is described. The paper is a purely theoretical argument over finite groups. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction. Structural facts about p-cores of centralizers give no cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, data distribution or loss. The Euler characteristic congruence is an exact algebraic fact about one group. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The product shuffle is a chain-level construction with a boundary identity. No objective function, feasible set or optimisation problem is posed. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Centralizer and faithfulness statements concern group actions on a simple component. They have no relation to nodes, messages, consensus or fault models. |
| other (computer algebra and formal verification of finite group properties) | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The theorem is a mathematical certificate that a finite group with trivial p-core has nonzero rational homology of its elementary abelian poset. It could serve as a sanity target for computer algebra systems checking small groups, but the paper provides no procedure. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **hyp-finite-group-any-prime** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated for finite groups and an arbitrary prime. Nothing is asserted for infinite, profinite or topological groups, nor for coefficient rings other than the stated rational ones. Blocked use: ['Applying the nonvanishing conclusion to infinite or topological groups', 'Reading the statement as a result about posets that are not subgroup posets of a finite group']

- **formal-scope-augmented-rational** (formal_scope): Homology is reduced and augmented with rational coefficients, so the empty poset carries a class in degree minus one. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Quillen's conjecture for all finite groups and all primes. More precisely, if a finite group <i>G</i> has trivial largest normal <i>p</i>-subgroup $`O_p(G)`$, then the poset of nontrivial elementary abelian <i>p</i>-subgroups of <i>G</i> has nonzero augmented reduced rational homology. This establishes the stronger rational-homology form of the conjecture.

| [stmt-thm-psl45-equivalent-poset](#stmt-thm-psl45-equivalent-poset) | theorem | `thm:psl45-equivalent-poset` | 35 | Let $G$ be a finite group, let $p$ be a prime, and let $L$ be a centerless component of $G$ with $p\mid &#124;L&#124;$. |
| [stmt-prop-psl45](#stmt-prop-psl45) | proposition | `prop:psl45` | 36 | A minimal-order counterexample to the rational homological implication at $p=2$ has no simple component isomorphic to $\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_4(5)$.* |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $G$ be a finite group and let $p$ be a prime. The poset $\mathcal A_p(G)$ consists of its nonidentity elementary abelian $p$-subgroups, ordered by inclusion. Thus each vertex is a subgroup isomorphic to $(\mathbb Z/p\mathbb
Z)^r$ for some $r\geq1$, and a simplex of its order complex is a strictly increasing chain of such subgroups. Write $O_p(G)$ for the largest normal $p$-subgroup of $G$.

Quillen’s conjecture asserts that contractibility of this order complex forces $O_p(G)\ne1$. It asks whether the topology of the elementary subgroups detects the existence of a normal $p$-subgroup. We prove a stronger statement.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every finite group $G$ and every prime $p$, if $O_p(G)=1$, then $$\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_p(G);\mathbb Q)\ne0.$$ Here reduced homology is augmented, so that the empty poset has $\widetilde H_{-1}(\varnothing;\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Q$.*

</div>

A contractible nonempty complex has zero reduced homology, and the empty complex is not contractible. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> therefore gives a positive resolution of Quillen’s conjecture, without a restriction on the prime or the composition factors of $G$.

The rational conclusion also determines acyclicity with other coefficients. Since the order complex is finite, a nonzero rational homology group comes from an integral homology group of positive free rank. The universal coefficient theorem therefore gives nonzero reduced homology over every field in the same degree; for the empty poset this follows directly from augmentation. Conversely, a nontrivial $p$-core makes $\mathcal A_p(G)$ contractible (Quillen 1978, Proposition 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Quillen1978](#ref-Quillen1978)]. Thus acyclicity over any fixed field characterizes the existence of a nontrivial normal $p$-subgroup.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Background.

Brown’s work on Euler characteristics introduced the poset $\mathcal S_p(G)$ of all nonidentity $p$-subgroups and related its Euler characteristic to the order of a Sylow subgroup (Brown 1975) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Brown1975](#ref-Brown1975)]. Quillen proved that the inclusion $\mathcal A_p(G)\subseteq\mathcal S_p(G)$ is a homotopy equivalence (Quillen 1978, Proposition 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Quillen1978](#ref-Quillen1978)], so the two posets carry the same homological information. He formulated the contractibility conjecture in 1978 and established it for solvable groups, groups of elementary abelian $p$-rank at most two, and groups of Lie type in defining characteristic (Quillen 1978, Conjecture 2.9, Proposition 2.10, Theorem 3.1, and Corollary 12.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Quillen1978](#ref-Quillen1978)]. For solvable groups with trivial $p$-core, his proof produces nonzero homology in the largest possible degree. This stronger conclusion, subsequently called the *Quillen dimension property*, became a central input to the component reductions.

The almost-simple case was established by Aschbacher and Kleidman (1990) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AschbacherKleidman1990](#ref-AschbacherKleidman1990)]. Passing from simple components to an arbitrary finite group requires control of their extensions and of the homology that survives in the ambient group. Aschbacher and Smith (1993) developed this approach for $p>5$, reducing the remaining problem to the dimension property for specified elementary extensions of unitary groups. Piterman and Smith (2025, Theorems 1.1 and 1.4) extended the reduction to all odd primes. Díaz Ramos (2026, Theorems B and C) proved the required unitary result and deduced the rational homological assertion for every finite group at odd primes. We retain a direct frame proof of the required unitary dimension property. Its local statement, Proposition <a href="#prop:unitary-odd" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:unitary-odd">23</a>, covers the indicated almost-simple groups with elementary abelian quotient, without assuming a splitting.

At two, successive component eliminations narrowed the remaining families. Piterman and Smith (2022, Corollary 1.3) showed that every component of a minimal counterexample must admit an outer automorphism of order two induced by the ambient group. Piterman (2024, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) established the dimension property for elementary $2$-extensions of exceptional Lie-type groups in odd characteristic, apart from a finite list in characteristic three. In a minimal counterexample, Piterman (2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Piterman2026](#ref-Piterman2026)] then eliminated the remaining Lie-type components in characteristic two. Combining these results with the preceding reductions gives the classical components in characteristic at least five listed in Piterman (2026, Theorem B) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Piterman2026](#ref-Piterman2026)]. We use this reduction in the precise form recalled in Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">2</a> and prove the remaining cases by our cycle and propagation constructions.

Several of the methods behind these reductions are also central to the present proof. Segev and Webb (1994) developed exact-sequence methods relating subgroup posets to a normal subgroup and its centralizers. Piterman (2021, secs. 3–5) developed full-chain homology propagation, maximal faithful elementary extensions, and deletion of overgroups, building on the propagation arguments of Aschbacher and Smith (1993) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[AschbacherSmith1993](#ref-AschbacherSmith1993)]. Our propagation proof spells out the coefficient and maximality conditions used by the constructions below. The constructible cycles of Díaz Ramos (2018) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DiazRamos2018](#ref-DiazRamos2018)] and the admissible collections of Díaz Ramos and Mazza (2022) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DiazRamosMazza2022](#ref-DiazRamosMazza2022)] likewise organize subgroup flags by separating nonzero simplex coefficients from vanishing boundary coefficients. The frame and decoration constructions here use this chain-level viewpoint, with quantitative estimates that are uniform as the dimension grows over a fixed field.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The cycle construction.

A frame is a decomposition of a finite-dimensional vector space into lines; when a form is present, the lines are nondegenerate and mutually orthogonal. Independent scalar signs on the lines give an elementary abelian projective subgroup. Its full subgroup flags support familiar apartment chains. We impose relations between frames that differ only inside a plane, so that the boundaries of their apartment chains cancel.

The main quantitative ingredient is a lower bound on the dimension of the resulting coefficient space, uniform as the dimension of the ambient space increases while the field stays fixed. This bound gives room to impose further equations: adjoining commuting elementary automorphisms creates new boundary faces, and their cancellation is a linear condition on the frame coefficients. Counting the possible completions of each partial choice bounds the number of such conditions. The comparison produces a nonzero cycle on full subgroup flags, rather than merely a nonzero collection of parameters describing apartments.

To obtain the dimension bound, we encode merger trees of frame lines by multilinear Lie superbrackets. A Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt argument and a bound for the actual kernel of the generator–relation complex turn elementary plane counts into the estimate of Theorem <a href="#thm:frame-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:frame-bound">11</a>. The supporting subspace and its isometry type are retained throughout, so the same estimate can be used after the descent imposed by specified commuting automorphisms. In the symmetric case with field parameter at least five, the retained fraction is at least $0.68^h$ in dimension $h$.

The geometric and algebraic ingredients have established precedents. The unitary decomposition poset is studied by Piterman and Welker (2024) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[PitermanWelker2024](#ref-PitermanWelker2024)]; the relation between merger trees and multilinear Lie representations appears in Stanley (1982; Robinson 2004) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Stanley1982](#ref-Stanley1982), [Robinson2004](#ref-Robinson2004)]. We identify the precise coefficient space needed here and retain the supporting-subspace grading in the PBW calculation. The generator–relation series method is related to that of Golod and Shafarevich (1964) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GolodShafarevich1964](#ref-GolodShafarevich1964)]; the additional kernel inequality is proved in Section <a href="#sec:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frames">4</a> before the logarithmic comparison is used.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### From a coefficient to homology.

The constructions may first give a cycle in an automorphism group larger than the chosen extension $H$ of a simple component $L$. Intersecting arbitrary subgroup flags with $H$ could collapse them or cancel their coefficients. Lemma <a href="#lem:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:restriction">19</a> avoids this by selecting a supported elementary group with largest intersection index, taking a residue at a fixed complement, and then intersecting the remaining flag with $H$. On that residue the operation is injective, so one nonzero full-flag coefficient survives.

At the prime two we work in a minimal counterexample and choose the last subgroup $A$ of maximal rank in a family of elementary subgroups generating faithful component extensions. Proper-subgroup induction supplies a nonbounding cycle in the centralizer of $H=LA$. Its product with the constructed cycle has a residue at the chosen flag that detects the centralizer class. The deletion argument in Lemma <a href="#lem:propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:propagation">9</a> ensures that this residue still detects nonbounding in the ambient group. This proves nonvanishing without requiring $A$ to have the largest possible elementary rank in $G$.

For odd primes the cited reduction requires a different conclusion: nonzero homology in the largest possible degree for certain unitary extensions. We establish the needed rank formula before constructing their cycles. This distinguishes the top-degree argument from the propagation argument used at two.

Two parts of the proof take shorter or different routes through these steps. The symplectic cycles are constructed directly in $H$ and need no restriction from a larger group. In the last branch for $\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_4(5)$, an equivalent-poset construction instead retains the join of the component and centralizer homology. Section <a href="#sec:exceptional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exceptional">9</a> states the precise conditions that lead to this branch.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">2</a> fixes conventions and states the reduction theorems. Section <a href="#sec:propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:propagation">3</a> proves the propagation lemma. Sections <a href="#sec:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:frames">4</a> and <a href="#sec:chains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:chains">5</a> develop frame cycles, their dimension bounds, and the decoration and restriction procedures. Sections <a href="#sec:linear" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:linear">6</a>–<a href="#sec:orthogonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:orthogonal">8</a> establish the required linear, unitary, symplectic, and orthogonal cases. Section <a href="#sec:exceptional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exceptional">9</a> treats the remaining small orthogonal case, realized as $\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_4(5)$, and Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">10</a> assembles the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:reductions"></a>
### Conventions and the reduction theorems

We first specify the homological statement used in induction and the classical groups to which the known reductions apply. Unless coefficients are specified otherwise, homology groups and chains have coefficients in $\mathbb Q$.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Posets, chains, and elementary rank

For a finite poset $P$, let $\Delta(P)$ be its order complex and write $\widetilde H_*(P)$ for its reduced homology. We use the augmented chain complex: there is a one-dimensional group in degree $-1$, generated by the empty simplex, and the boundary of each vertex is that generator. Consequently $\widetilde H_{-1}(\varnothing)=\mathbb Q$. For a nonempty finite complex, nonvanishing in degree $-1$ is impossible.

A *full flag* in an elementary abelian group $E$ of rank $r$ is a chain $$E_1<E_2<\cdots<E_r=E,
\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rk}}E_i=i.$$ We also call such a flag saturated. A coefficient at a full flag always means its coefficient as an oriented simplex, with the displayed increasing order. Write $$m_p(G)=\max\{\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}E:E\leq G\text{ an elementary abelian }p\text{-subgroup}\},$$ allowing $E=1$ in this maximum. The order complex of $\mathcal A_p(G)$ has dimension $m_p(G)-1$, including the empty case. We say that $G$ has the *Quillen dimension property* at $p$ if <a id="eq:QD"></a>$$\label{eq:QD}
\widetilde H_{m_p(G)-1}(\mathcal A_p(G))\ne0.$$ A nonzero cycle in that degree cannot be a boundary, because the chain group in the next degree is zero.

<a id="eq:HQC"></a>
The *homological implication* for $(G,p)$ is $$\label{eq:HQC}
O_p(G)=1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\widetilde H_*(\mathcal A_p(G))\ne0.$$ When arguing by minimality we fix $p$ and minimize $|G|$ among counterexamples to <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a>. Thus every group of smaller order satisfies <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a>, with no restriction on its composition factors. If $p\nmid|G|$, its elementary poset is empty and it is not a counterexample under our convention.

We use two standard elementary facts about finite posets. Pointwise comparable order-preserving maps induce homotopic maps on order complexes. Also, deleting a vertex whose strict upper interval is contractible preserves homotopy type: its link is the join of its lower and upper intervals and is contractible. These facts also follow from the standard fiber and gluing lemmas for posets; see Quillen (1978; Piterman and Smith 2025) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Quillen1978](#ref-Quillen1978), [PitermanSmith2025](#ref-PitermanSmith2025)].

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Components and automorphisms

A component of a finite group is a subnormal quasisimple subgroup. The components supplied by the reduction below are simple. Distinct components commute; distinct conjugates of a simple component intersect trivially and generate their direct product. We use these standard component facts and the usual automorphism structure of finite classical simple groups; see Gorenstein, Lyons, and Solomon (1998) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GLS1998](#ref-GLS1998)].

If $L$ is nonabelian simple, a subgroup containing $L$ and acting faithfully on $L$ by conjugation is identified with a subgroup of $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$. An *elementary $p$-extension* of $L$ means a split extension $L\rtimes B$ in which $B$ is elementary abelian and induces only outer automorphisms; $B=1$ is allowed. In particular, an elementary quotient alone is not being taken as a splitting hypothesis. In our configurations, $H=LA$ with $A$ elementary and $H$ faithful on $L$; a vector-space complement to $A\cap L$ in $A$ supplies the required subgroup $B$.

Every such faithful extension $L\le H\le\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(L)$ has $O_p(H)=1$. Indeed, for $Q=O_p(H)$ simplicity gives $Q\cap L=1$, and normality gives $[Q,L]\le Q\cap L$. Hence $Q\le C_H(L)=1$. Thus these extensions meet the trivial-core condition in the conditional dimension-property convention of the cited reductions.

For the linear and unitary groups, the parameter $q$ is the order of the base field: the natural unitary space is over $\mathbb F_{q^2}$. In the orthogonal sections, the discriminant means the determinant square class of a Gram matrix. Its relation to the Witt sign includes the usual dimension-dependent factor. These two conventions will not be interchanged.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The established reductions

<a id="stmt-thm-reduction-two"></a>
<div id="thm:reduction-two" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Reduction at two). *Let $G$ be a minimal-order counterexample to <a href="#eq:HQC" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:HQC">[eq:HQC]</a> for $p=2$. Then $G$ has a simple component $L$ in the following list, where the field parameter $q$ has characteristic at least five: $$\begin{array}{ll}
\mathop{\mathrm{PSL}}_n(q),\ \mathop{\mathrm{PSU}}_n(q)& n\geq4,\\
\mathop{\mathrm{PSp}}_{2n}(q)& n\geq3,\\
\Omega_{2n+1}(q)& n\geq2,\\
